(b) Any function performed by work now or heretofore done by employees 
subject to this agreement shall continue to be work subject to this agreement 
and done by employees covered by this agreement, irrespective of any change 
in the means or methods by which such function or work is performed.

### T0543ae54705790f6fc00f72a

Text hash 7632bf117fda5491a3bd48512a4c01a22fa04ffaa0177eb796cfa3d9bc0c3c4b; absolute characters 9565–10059; files: jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_11011_Actual_date_of_the_Emergency_Board_Trans_World_Airlines_and_Flight_Engineers_Internat_38fc05b9d9.txt

```text
At the present time the parties operate under a "Basic Agree- 
ment," together with various "Supplement,] Agreements," "Letters of 
Agreement~" and "Memoranda of Understanding." The current basic 
agreement was sighted July 29, 1958, effective from that date until 
January 1, 1961, and from year to year thereafter unless reopened 
through written notice served in accordance with section 6, title I, 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, at least 60 days prior to Janu- 
ary 1st of any year.
```

### T05d0217283d20e753a79bf3a

Text hash 8f317073674b7bd6b9384c4f44defcf69948cdf45725a38543b4f16c10e0fc59; absolute characters 1375–1734; files: jobs/clause-drafts/output/texts/cd-job-fr-originals-10904-52b1d23cb4.txt

```text
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by carriers represented by the 
New York Harbor Carriers’ Conference 
Committee, or by their employees, in the 
conditions out of which the dispute arose.
```

Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 11008
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD TO INVESTIGATE DISPUTE BE­
TWEEN THE AKRON &  BARBERTON BELT RAILROAD COMPANY AND 
OTHER CARRIERS AND CERTAIN OF THEIR EMPLOYEES
W H E R E A S a dispute exists between the Akron & Barberton Belt 
Railroad Company and other carriers represented by the Eastern, 
Western and Southeastern Carriers’ Conference Committees, desig­
nated in List A attached hereto and made a part hereof, and certain 
of their employees represented by the Eleven Cooperating Railway 
Labor Organizations, labor organizations, designated in List B  at­
tached hereto and made a part hereof; and ,
W H E R E A S this dispute has not heretofore been adjusted under 
the provisions of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; and
W H E R E A S this dispute, in the judgment of the National Media­
tion Board, threatens substantially to interrupt interstate commerce 
to a degree such as to deprive the country of essential transportation 
service:
NOW, T H E R E F O R E , by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), 
I  hereby create a board of three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate this dispute. No member of the board shall be pecuni­
arily or otherwise interested in any organization of railroad employees 
or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to the President with respect 
to the dispute within thirty days from the date of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, 
from this date and for thirty days after the board has made its report 
to the President, no change, except by agreement, shall be made by the 
Akron & Barberton Belt Railroad Company and other carriers rep­
resented by the Eastern, Western and Southeastern Carriers’ Con­
ference Committee, or by their employees, in the conditions out of 
which the dispute arose.

E xcess  Acreage Utilization  Date
Basis and purpose.  The amendment  
herein is issued pursuant to and in ac­
cordance with the Agricultural Adjust­
ment Act of 1938, as amended, and is  
issued for the purpose of amending the  
date for the disposal of excess wheat  
acreage in five counties in Kansas. Since  
the determination of 1962 wheat acreage  
w ill soon be made, it is important that  
State and county committees be notified  
of the amendment herein as soon as pos­
sible so that producers with 1962 excess  
wheat acreage may be notified of the  
nnal date for utilization of such excess  
acreage as wheat cover crop. Accord­
ingly, it is hereby found that compliance  
with the public notice, procedure and  
30-day effective date provisions of sec­
tion 4 of the Administrative Procedure  
A c^ s  impracticable and contrary to the 
Public interest. Therefore, the amend­
m en t shall become effective upon its  
publication in the F ederal R egister .
Paragraph (b) of §  728.1145 is  
amended to change the date of May 20  
to June 1 for five counties in Kansas as  
follows: Cheyenne, Decatur, Rawlins,  
Sherman, and Thomas.
(Secs. 374, 375, 52 Stat. 65, 66, as amended;  
68 Stat. 904, 7 U.S.C. 1374, 1375)

eral R egister .

ary 28, 1962.

tural Stabilization and Con­
servation Service.

Title 14-AERONAUTICS AND 
SPACE
Chapter III— Federal Aviation Agency 
SUBCHAPTER C— AIRCRAFT REGULATIONS  
[Reg. Docket No. 1088; Amdt. 403]
PART 507— AIRWORTHINESS 
DIRECTIVES
Aero Commander Model 680F Aircraft
As a result of several failures of the  
alternate induction air valve and shaft
on Aero Commander Model 680F air­
craft which caused loss of engine power,  
an airworthiness directive is considered  
necessary requiring replacement of the  
original parts with improved parts.
As a situation exists which demands  
immediate action in the interest of  
safety, it is found that notice and public  
procedure hereon are impracticable and  
good cause exists for making this amend­
ment effective upon publication in the  
F ederal R egister .
In consideration of the foregoing, and  
pursuant to the authority delegated to  
me by the Administrator (25 F.R. 6489),  
§  507.10(a) of Part 507 (14 CFR Part  
507), is hereby amended by adding the  
following new airworthiness directive:
Aero C ommander . Applies to all Model 680F  
aircraft, including pressurized versions,  
with. Serial Numbers 871 through 1170.
Compliance required within th,e next 25  
hours’ time in service after the effective date  
of this directive.
In order to preclude failure of the alter­
nate induction air valve and shaft assembly  
resulting in loss of engine power, replace the  
original air valve and shaft and rig in ac­
cordance with instructions outlined in Aero  
Commander Service Bulletin No. 76A dated  
February 5, 1962, or FAA approved equiv­
alent.
This amendment shall become ef­
fective March 6,1962.
(Sec. 313(a), 601, 603; 72 Stat. 752, 775, 776;  
49 U.S.C. 1354(a), 1421,1423)
Issued in Washington, D.C., on Febru­
ary 26,1962.
G. S. M oore, 
Acting Director,  
Flight Standards Service.
[F.R. Doc. 62-2157; Filed, Mar. 5, 1962;
8:45 ajn.]
SUBCHAPTER E— AIR NAVIGATION  
REGULATIONS
[Airspace Docket No. 62-WA-19]
PART 601— DESIGNATION OF CON­
TROLLED A IRSPACE, REPORTING  
POINTS, POSITIVE CONTROL ROUTE 
SEGMENTS, AND POSITIVE CON­
TROL AREAS
Alteration of Control Area Extension
The purpose of this amendment to  
§  601.1479 of the regulations of the Ad­
ministrator is to alter the Peotone, HI.,  
control area extension.
The Peotone control area extension is  
currently described as that airspace  
southeast of Poetone bounded on the east  
by VOR Federal airway No. 7, on the  
southwest by VOR Federal airway No.  
227, on the west by VOR Federal airway  
No. 53 and on the north by VOR Federal  
airway No. 38. Victor 227 has been re­
designated as VOR Federal airway No.  
491 (14 CFR 600.6491). Therefore, in  
order to correctly describe the Peotone  
control area extension, action is taken  
herein to substitute Victor 491 for Victor
2147

Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive O rder 10919
CREATING AN EM ERGEN CY BOARD  
TO  INVESTIGATE A  DISPUTE BE­
TWEEN THE PAN AMERICAN  
WORLD AIRW AYS, INC., AND CER­
TAIN OF ITS EMPLOYEES
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
the Pan American World Airways, Inc., 
a carrier, and certain of its employees 
represented by the Plight Engineers’  In ­
ternational Association, PAA Chapter, a 
labor organization; and 
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and ____
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate nnrtimfirp.fi to a degree such as to 
deprive the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in  me by section 10 O f 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U.S.C. 160) , 3 hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate this dispute. No member of 
the board shall be pecuniarily or other­
wise interested in any organization of 
airline employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the dispute 
within thirty days from the date of this 
order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by the Pan American World Air­
ways, Inc., or by its employees, in the 
conditions out of which the dispute arose.
J ohn  F . K ennedy
T he  W hite  H ouse ,
February 17, 1961.

Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 11040
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD TO INVESTIGATE DISPUTE
BETWEEN THE BELT RAILWAY COMPANY OF CHICAGO AND
CERTAIN OF ITS EMPLOYEES
W HEREAS a dispute exists between The Belt Railway Company 
of Chicago, a carrier, and certain of its employees represented by 
the Brotherhood of Locomotive Engineers, a labor organization; and
W HEREAS this dispute has not heretofore been adjusted under 
the provisions of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; and
W HEREAS this dispute, in the judgment of the National Media­
tion Board, threatens substantially to interrupt interstate commerce 
to a degree such as to deprive a section of the country of essential trans­
portation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), I  
hereby create a board of three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate this dispute. No member of the board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organization of railroad employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to the President with respect to 
this dispute within thirty days from the date of-this order.
As provided by Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, 
from this date and for thirty days after the board has made its report 
to the President, no change, except by agreement, shall be made by The 
Belt Railway Company of Chicago, or by its employees, in the con­
ditions out of which this dispute arose.
T he W h ite H ouse,
August 6 , 1962.
J ohn  F. K ennedy
[F.R. Doc. 62-8013 ; Filed, Aug. 7,1962 ; 3 :50 p.m.]

Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 11011
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD TO INVESTIGATE A DISPUTE BE­
TWEEN THE TRANS WORLD AIRLINES, INC., AND CERTAIN OF ITS
EMPLOYEES
W HEREAS a dispute exists between the Trans World Airlines, 
Inc., a carrier, and certain of its employees represented by the Flight 
Engineers’  International Association, AFL-CIO, a labor organiza­
tion ; and
W HEREAS this dispute has.not heretofore been adjusted under the 
provisions of the Railway Labor Act, as amended ; and
W HEREAS this dispute, in the judgment of the National Media­
tion Board, threatens substantially to interrupt interstate commerce to 
a degree such as to deprive a section of the country of essential trans­
portation servicé:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me by 
Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), I  
hereby create a board of three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate this dispute. No member of the board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organization of airline employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to the President with respect 
to the dispute within thirty days from the date of this order.
As provided by section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, 
from this date and for thirty days after the board has made its report 
to the President, no change, except by agreement, shall be made by the 
Trans World Airlines, Inc. or by its employees, in the conditions out 
of which the dispute arose.
J o h n F. K en n e d y
T h e W h it e H ouse ,

tiles and textile products and also only in respect of “Arragements re­
garding international trade in cotton textiles”, done at Geneva July
21,1961.
The Secretary of the Treasury is authorized to administer the regu­
lations issued by him under the foregoing provisions of this letter.

Executive Order 10926
AMENDING EXECUTIVE ORDER NO.  
10919,1  FEBRUARY 17, 1961, TO  
EXTEND THE TIME WITHIN WHICH  
THE BOARD CREATED THEREIN,  
TO INVESTIGATE A DISPUTE BE­
TWEEN PAN AMERICAN WORLD  
AIRWAYS, INC., AND CERTAIN OF  
ITS EMPLOYEES, SHALL REPORT  
ITS FINDINGS TO THE PRESIDENT
By virtue of the authority vested in 
me by section 10 of the Railway Labor 
Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), and as 
President of the United States, I hereby 
extend until June 20, 1961, the time 
within which the emergency board cre­
ated by Executive Order No. 10919 of 
February 17, 1961, to investigate a dis­
pute between Pan American World 
Airways, Inc., and certain of its em­
ployees, represented by the Flight En­
gineers’  International Association, PAA 
Chapter, a labor organization, shall re­
port its findings to the President.
```

12, 1962 FEDERAL REGISTER 5533
Executive Order 11027
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD TO INVESTIGATE A DISPUTE  
BETWEEN THE NEW YORK CENTRAL RAILROAD COMPANY SYSTEM  
AND THE PITTSBURGH AND LAKE ERIE RAILROAD COMPANY AND  
CERTAIN OF THEIR EMPLOYEES
W HEREAS a dispute exists between the New York Central Rail­
road Company System and the Pittsburgh and Lake Erie Railroad 
Company and certain of their employees represented by the Order 
of Railroad Telegraphers; and
W HEREAS this dispute has not heretofore been adjusted under the 
provisions of the Railway Labor Act, as amended ; and
W HEREAS this dispute, in the judgment of the National Media­
tion Board, threatens substantially to interrupt interstate commerce 
to a degree such as to deprive a section of the country of essential 
transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the authority vested in me 
by Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 U.S.C. 160), 
I  hereby create a board of three members, to be appointed by me, to 
investigate this dispute. No member of the board shall be pecuniarily 
or otherwise interested in any organization of railroad employees or 
any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to the President with respect to 
this dispute within thirty days from the date of this order.
As provided by Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, as amended, 
from this date and for thirty days after the board has made its re­
port to the President, no change, except by agreement, shall be made 
by the New York Central Railroad Company System and the Pitts­
burgh and Lake Erie Railroad Company, or by their employees, in 
the conditions out of which this dispute arose.
J ohn  F. K ennedy
T he  W h ite H ouse,
June 8,1962.
[F.R. Doc. 62-5802 ; Filed, June 11,1962 ; 10:50 a.m.]

```

Presidential Documents
Title 3— THE PRESIDENT
Executive Order 10904
CREATING AN EMERGENCY BOARD  
TO INVESTIGATE A DISPUTE BE­
TWEEN CERTAIN CARRIERS REP­
RESENTED BY THE NEW YORK  
HARBOR CARRIERS’ CONFERENCE  
COMMITTEE AND CERTAIN OF  
THEIR EMPLOYEES
WHEREAS a dispute exists between 
certain carriers represented by the New 
York Harbor Carriers’  Conference Com­
mittee, designated in the List of Carriers 
attached hereto and hereby made a part 
hereof, and certain of their employees 
represented by the Lighter Captains’  
Union, Local 996, I.L.A., a labor organi­
zation; and
WHEREAS this dispute has not here­
tofore been adjusted under the provisions 
of the Railway Labor Act, as amended; 
and
WHEREAS this dispute, in the judg­
ment of the National Mediation Board, 
threatens substantially to interrupt in­
terstate commerce to a degree such as to 
deprive a section of the country of es­
sential transportation service:
NOW, THEREFORE, by virtue of the 
authority vested in me by section 10 of 
the Railway Labor Act, as amended (45 
U.S.C. 160) , I hereby create a board of 
three members, to be appointed by me, 
to investigate this dispute. No member 
of the board shall be pecuniarily or 
otherwise interested in any organization 
of railroad employees or any carrier.
The board shall report its findings to 
the President with respect to the dispute 
within thirty days from the date of this 
order.
As provided by section 10 of the Rail­
way Labor Act, as amended, from this 
date and for thirty days after the board 
has made its report to the President, no 
change, except by agreement, shall be 
made by carriers represented by the 
New York Harbor Carriers’ Conference 
Committee, or by their employees, in the 
conditions out of which the dispute arose.
D wight  D. Eisenhower
The White  House ,
January 12,1961.
List  of C arriers
The Baltimore &  Ohio Railroad Company 
The Central Railroad Company of New Jersey  
The Erie -Lackawanna Railroad Company 
The Lehigh Valley Railroad Company  
The New York Central Railroad Company 
The New York, New Haven &  Hartford Rail­
road Company
The Pennsylvania Railroad Company

SATION AND CREDIT GRANTED  
CERTAIN EMPLOYEES OF FEDERAL

UNITED STATES
Subpart E— -U.nhealthful Posts  
Additions  to L ist

Service Act of 1946, as amended, and

dated June 27, 1951, as amended, the

graph of Executive Order No. 6942 of 
January 8, 1935, Executive Order No. 
7062 of June 5, 1935, Executive Order

amended, and Departmental Regulations

Title 7— AGRICULTURE
Chapter IV— Federal Crop Insurance  
Corporation, Department of Agri­
culture
[Arndt. 10]
PART 401—  FEDERAL CROP  
INSURANCE
Subpart— Regulations for the 1961  
and Succeeding Crop Years  
P each C rop, 1961
The above-identified regulations are 
amended effective beginning with the 
1961 crop year as follows :
1. The table following paragraph (a) 
of §  401.3 of this chapter is amended by 
inserting the following statement imme­
diately following the closing date of Ja n ­
uary 15 for peaches for all states:
Applications for peach insurance for the  
1961 crop year submitted during the period  
January 16 through January 31, 1961, when  
accompanied by payment of the premium  
may be accepted by the Corporation.
2. The peach endorsement shown in 
§  401.26 of this chapter is amended by 
adding a section 14 thereto to read as 
follows:
14. Payment of premium during the period  
January 16 through January 31, 1961.  Not­
withstanding any provision of the contract  
the provisions of this section 14 shall be a  
part of the peach endorsement of any con­
tract of insurance for the 1961 crop year for  
which premium payment is made during the  
period January 16 through January 81, 1961.  
Premium payment may be made during the  
period January 16 through January 31, 1961,  
and in any case where the premium is paid  
during such period the date January 81 shall  
be substituted for the date January 15 wher­
ever it appears in sections 2, 5, 11, and 12(c)  
of this endorsement; and the date February  
1 shall be substituted for the date January  
16 wherever it appears in sections 7 and 12  
(a) of this endorsement.
(Secs. 506, 516, 52 Stat. 73, as amended, 77,  
as amended; 7 U.S.C. 1506, 1516)
Adopted by the Board of Directors on 
January 12, 1961.
[seal] F. N. McCartney,
Secretary,
Federal Crop Insurance Corporation.
Approved: January 12, 1961.
Clarence L. M iller,
Assistant Secretary.
[F.R. Doc. 61-361; Filed, Jan. 13, 1961;

Chapter IX— Agricultural Marketing  
Service (Marketing Agreements and  
Orders), Department of Agriculture  
[Navel Orange Reg. 201 ]
PART 914— NAVEL ORANGES
GROWN IN ARIZONA AND DES­
IGNATED PART OF CALIFORNIA
Limitation of Handling  
§ 914.501 Navel Orange Regulation 201.
(a) Findings.  (1) Pursuant to the 
marketing agreement, as amended, and 
Order No. 14, as amended (7 CFR P art 
914), regulating the handling of navel 
orangés grown in Arizona and desig­
nated part of California, effective under 
the applicable provisions of the Agricul­
tural Marketing Agreement Act of 1937, 
as amended (7 U.S.C. 601-674), and upon 
the basis of the recommendations and 
information submitted by the Navel 
Orange  Administrative Committee, 
established under the said amended 
marketing agreement and order, and 
upon other available information, it is 
hereby found th at the limitation of han­
dling of such navel oranges, as herein­
after provided, will tend to effectuate th e 
declared policy of the act by tending to  
establish and maintain such orderly i  
marketing conditions for such oranges 
as will provide, in the interests of p ro ­
ducers and consumers, and orderly flo w  
of the supply thereof to market througn- :  
out the normal marketing season to  
avoid unreasonable fluctuations in su p ­
plies and prices, and is not for the pur­
pose of maintaining prices to fanners 
above the level which it is declared to b e  
the policy of Congress to establish under 
the act.
(2) It is hereby further found that it 
is impracticable and contrary to the 
public interest to give preliminary n o tice, 
engage in public rule-making procedure, 
and postpone the effective date of th is 
section until 30 days after publication

APPOINTED BY EXECUTIVE ORDER 10904 DATED

To Investigate a dispute between Certain Carriers represented 
by the New York Harbor Carriers' Conference Committee 
and Lighter Captains' Union, Local 996, International Long-

MR. PRESIDENT : The Emergency Board created on January 125 19615 
by Executive Order 10904, pursuant to Section 10 of the Railway

riers represented by the New York Harbor Carriers' Conference Com- 
mittee, and certain of their employees represented by the Lighter

honor to submit herewith its report and recommendations based upon

The dossier’s conflicts and missing lists are empty or concern only a number citation without a change provision (report dated 1961-03-06 in the held NMB text; thirty days later is 1961-04-05).\",\"independent_command_check\":\"No independent command survives: the board’s creation was performed when the order issued; the report direction and the status-quo period are time-limited by the order itself and by section 10 of the Railway Labor Act; the order creates no standing office, delegation, rule or continuing duty that would outlast the report and the thirty-day period. I read the whole order; the representation is the machine-extracted text layer of the official PDF (OCR for this period), and I did not inspect the page images. End: express revocation by Executive Order 12553 on 1986-02-25, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines (option C): a report deadline and a spent standstill are not ends. Searches: current-law:usc, current-law:ecfr and later-events:ofr_catalog, each marked complete in the dossier; the U.S. Code and eCFR searches found no hit naming this order. The only later instrument other than Executive Order 12553 that names this order in the held texts is Executive Order 10926, recorded as a later event.\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"The order contains no fixed end date and no termination clause. Its commands are a bar on interested board members, a requirement that the board report within thirty days from the date of the order (due by March 19, 1961), and a standstill running from the date of the order until thirty days after the report. The report deadline and the standstill are spent facts, not ends, under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, and neither is claimed as the end. A held lead copy of the report of Emergency Board No. 135 (a text of the front matter of the National Mediation Board publication, graded held_text_lead, a grade the claim format does not accept as a cited source) is dated June 20, 1961. Thirty days after June 20, 1961 is July 20, 1961. Those dates come from a lead and are disclosed, not claimed as events or as the end: the clause is described only as functionally spent on June 20, 1961, the report date, with the standstill functionally spent on July 20, 1961. My reading of the held texts of Executive Order 12553 and the number search of the held texts show no earlier revoking, superseding or terminating instrument for this order; the instrument recorded as a later event only extends the board's reporting time.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"Each command (create the board, bar interested members, require the report, freeze conditions) depends on this order and on the dispute it created; none is a separate delegation, continuing regulation or standing rule. The standstill sentence applies section 10 of the Railway Labor Act (45 U.S.C. 160); the statute is a separate source of law that an Executive order does not revoke, but the periods measured here were set by this order's creation of the board, and no held claim-grade source shows them still running when the order was revoked.\",\"savings_check\":\"Executive Order 12553 was read in full: it consists of one revoking sentence, a table of the revoked orders by number and subject, an editorial note and the signature block, and it contains no saving or transition provision. No residual effect of this order is shown to depend on its continued force. Section 10 of the Railway Labor Act continues independently as stated in the independent-command check.\",\"scope_check\":\"One clause is inventoried: the operative text from the order heading through the standstill sentence (the recitals, the creation of the board of three members, the bar on interested members, the report requirement and the standstill). The signature, the date line and any attached lists follow it; the attached lists, where present, only identify carriers and labor organizations and contain no command. Executive Order 12553 revokes the order by number as a whole, not in part, so every command in the clause ends on the same date. The ending source cited is the revoking sentence and table of Executive Order 12553.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:10919#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:10919#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"638a6a29ea554292b91398ec7964e77dab398419bf3777f1d5ea8f8af7e66645","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10926.txt","sha256":"638a6a29ea554292b91398ec7964e77dab398419bf3777f1d5ea8f8af7e66645","state":"match"},"sha256":"9e57245c6e3141acd9b2ae498cb39500146348a1fa63abbb3d4fbfd2ea8113b7","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10926.txt","text_sha256":"638a6a29ea554292b91398ec7964e77dab398419bf3777f1d5ea8f8af7e66645"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"68d4cfc3d168f13f20920015df2050c96f055210549b645c55365707f8f68e4d","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10919.txt","sha256":"68d4cfc3d168f13f20920015df2050c96f055210549b645c55365707f8f68e4d","state":"match"},"sha256":"c989a6616bc2b5689ef17a8383fc5abd688db3f15b180ca166ccac69f2812768","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_10919.txt","text_sha256":"68d4cfc3d168f13f20920015df2050c96f055210549b645c55365707f8f68e4d"}]}]}

Kennedy) dated 1962-02-22; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Creating an emergency board to investigate a dispute between Eastern Air Lines, Inc., and certain of its employees). I read the whole clause and the signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers the recitals and every operative sentence; nothing outside it is a command (any attached lists of carriers or unions identify parties and are not commands). The one operative clause creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, 45 U.S.C. 160, to investigate the dispute involving Eastern Air Lines, Inc., and certain of its employees represented by the Flight Engineers' International Association, E.A.L. Chapter; it bars board members from pecuniary or other interest in any carrier or employee organization; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and it recites the statutory standstill: from the date of the order and for thirty days after the board reports, no change except by agreement is to be made in the conditions out of which the dispute arose. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" and its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11006 Creating an emergency board to investigate an airline labor dispute\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. The thirty-day report deadline and the standstill that runs to thirty days after the report are periods measured by the order's own terms. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, neither the report nor the end of the standstill ends the order; each is a spent fact, and the order stayed on the books until it was expressly revoked. The order was functionally spent once the board had reported and the standstill had run, but this claim assigns no date to that point because no report date is cited from a registered source here; a board report held as a lead-grade Government printing is not cited, and a dated transmittal letter is not proof of receipt or of standstill expiry. Earlier candidates: a search of the held order texts for citations of this order by number found no later order that revokes, amends or extends it; the Office of the Federal Register catalog notes name EO 12553 as the revoking order. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception, and the order created no continuing right or obligation outside its own terms. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms periods (report deadline and standstill) are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21. No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession, expiry or termination. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. Later orders that merely recite or list this order would not be ends; none was found that does more than EO 12553.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the board: the standstill is recited from the statute and depends on the board's creation and report. No other command in the order, and no other order that incorporates it, was found in the held texts.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception (searched as described); the board's report and any settlement or later legislation are not obligations created by this order and are not claimed as surviving effects.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, whole order. Kennedy) dated 1962-03-03; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Creating an emergency board to investigate dispute between the Akron & Barberton Belt Railroad Company and other carriers and certain of their employees). I read the whole clause and the signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers the recitals and every operative sentence; nothing outside it is a command (any attached lists of carriers or unions identify parties and are not commands). The one operative clause creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, 45 U.S.C. 160, to investigate the dispute involving the Akron & Barberton Belt Railroad Company and other carriers represented by the Eastern, Western and Southeastern Carriers' Conference Committees (List A) and certain of their employees represented by the Eleven Cooperating Railway Labor Organizations (List B); it bars board members from pecuniary or other interest in any carrier or employee organization; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and it recites the statutory standstill: from the date of the order and for thirty days after the board reports, no change except by agreement is to be made in the conditions out of which the dispute arose. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" and its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11008 Creating an emergency board to investigate railroad labor disputes\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. The thirty-day report deadline and the standstill that runs to thirty days after the report are periods measured by the order's own terms. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, neither the report nor the end of the standstill ends the order; each is a spent fact, and the order stayed on the books until it was expressly revoked. The order was functionally spent once the board had reported and the standstill had run, but this claim assigns no date to that point because no report date is cited from a registered source here; a board report held as a lead-grade Government printing is not cited, and a dated transmittal letter is not proof of receipt or of standstill expiry. Earlier candidates: a search of the held order texts for citations of this order by number found no later order that revokes, amends or extends it; the Office of the Federal Register catalog notes name EO 12553 as the revoking order. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception, and the order created no continuing right or obligation outside its own terms. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms periods (report deadline and standstill) are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21. No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession, expiry or termination. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. Later orders that merely recite or list this order would not be ends; none was found that does more than EO 12553.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the board: the standstill is recited from the statute and depends on the board's creation and report. No other command in the order, and no other order that incorporates it, was found in the held texts.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception (searched as described); the board's report and any settlement or later legislation are not obligations created by this order and are not claimed as surviving effects.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, whole order. The dossier's inventory spans the recitals, the creation of the board, the report command and the statutory standstill recital to the signature; I confirm it as complete. EO 12553 revokes the whole order (it lists the order itself and its amendments), so no part of the order is left outside the Kennedy) dated 1962-03-20; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Creating an emergency board to investigate a dispute between the Trans World Airlines, Inc., and certain of its employees). I read the whole clause and the signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers the recitals and every operative sentence; nothing outside it is a command (any attached lists of carriers or unions identify parties and are not commands). The one operative clause creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, 45 U.S.C. 160, to investigate the dispute involving Trans World Airlines, Inc., and certain of its employees; it bars board members from pecuniary or other interest in any carrier or employee organization; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and it recites the statutory standstill: from the date of the order and for thirty days after the board reports, no change except by agreement is to be made in the conditions out of which the dispute arose. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" and its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11011 Creating an emergency board to investigate an airline labor dispute\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. The thirty-day report deadline and the standstill that runs to thirty days after the report are periods measured by the order's own terms. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, neither the report nor the end of the standstill ends the order; each is a spent fact, and the order stayed on the books until it was expressly revoked. The order was functionally spent once the board had reported and the standstill had run, but this claim assigns no date to that point because no report date is cited from a registered source here; a board report held as a lead-grade Government printing is not cited, and a dated transmittal letter is not proof of receipt or of standstill expiry. Earlier candidates: a search of the held order texts for citations of this order by number found no later order that revokes, amends or extends it; the Office of the Federal Register catalog notes name EO 12553 as the revoking order. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception, and the order created no continuing right or obligation outside its own terms. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms periods (report deadline and standstill) are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21. No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession, expiry or termination. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. Later orders that merely recite or list this order would not be ends; none was found that does more than EO 12553.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the board: the standstill is recited from the statute and depends on the board's creation and report. No other command in the order, and no other order that incorporates it, was found in the held texts.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception (searched as described); the board's report and any settlement or later legislation are not obligations created by this order and are not claimed as surviving effects.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, whole order. The dossier's inventory spans the recitals, the creation of the board, the report command and the statutory standstill recital to the signature; I confirm it as complete. EO 12553 revokes the whole order (it lists the order itself and its amendments), so no part of the order is left outside the ending.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:5","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:5","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11011#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11011#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"c4d8cb4e48f296281afbb05b02a586ac540d92feb4aeb3c5748ddc33f2da7466","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11011.txt","sha256":"c4d8cb4e48f296281afbb05b02a586ac540d92feb4aeb3c5748ddc33f2da7466","state":"match"},"sha256":"75ad08fa5897605f2bf9688b7a08ffa04ac634d5d8c2763280e680d3d949867c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11011.txt","text_sha256":"c4d8cb4e48f296281afbb05b02a586ac540d92feb4aeb3c5748ddc33f2da7466"}]}]}

Kennedy) dated 1962-06-08; the number, subject and date agree with the census register row (subject: Creating an emergency board to investigate a dispute between the New York Central Railroad Company System and the Pittsburgh and Lake Erie Railroad Company and certain of their employees). I read the whole clause and the signature block. Inventory: the dossier's single whole-order clause covers the recitals and every operative sentence; nothing outside it is a command (any attached lists of carriers or unions identify parties and are not commands). The one operative clause creates an emergency board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act, 45 U.S.C. 160, to investigate the dispute involving the New York Central Railroad Company System and the Pittsburgh and Lake Erie Railroad Company and certain of their employees; it bars board members from pecuniary or other interest in any carrier or employee organization; it directs the board to report its findings to the President within thirty days from the date of the order; and it recites the statutory standstill: from the date of the order and for thirty days after the board reports, no change except by agreement is to be made in the conditions out of which the dispute arose. Ending: EO 12553 of February 25, 1986, titled \"Revocation of Various Executive Orders\", orders that the listed Executive Orders, and \"any amendments thereto, are revoked:\" and its table lists this order by number and subject on one line: \"11027 Creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute\". EO 12553 contains no effective-date wording (the held text was searched for effective, take effect, shall become, except, notwithstanding, nothing in, provided that and saving, with no hit outside table subjects); it is headed as an order of February 25, 1986 and signed that day, so the date is read as 1986-02-25, the signing date. The alternative reading is the Federal Register publication date, 1986-03-03 (Monday, March 3, 1986, 51 FR 7237); no later date is supported by the text. The quoted source for the end is the enacting paragraph of EO 12553, which names no order; the order is named by its row in the No./Subject table, quoted above, so the table row and the enacting paragraph are read together. The thirty-day report deadline and the standstill that runs to thirty days after the report are periods measured by the order's own terms. Under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21 on report deadlines, neither the report nor the end of the standstill ends the order; each is a spent fact, and the order stayed on the books until it was expressly revoked. The order was functionally spent once the board had reported and the standstill had run, but this claim assigns no date to that point because no report date is cited from a registered source here; a board report held as a lead-grade Government printing is not cited, and a dated transmittal letter is not proof of receipt or of standstill expiry. Earlier candidates: a search of the held order texts for citations of this order by number found no later order that revokes, amends or extends it; the Office of the Federal Register catalog notes name EO 12553 as the revoking order. Savings: EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception, and the order created no continuing right or obligation outside its own terms. Searches selected after reading their coverage notes (all three complete, no coverage warning): U.S. Code release point 119-121 (no hit), eCFR as of 2026-10-14 (no hit), and the Federal Register catalog disposition notes (2 machine hits, the revocation note on this order and the matching note on EO 12553).\nMember checks: {\"earlier_end_check\":\"Own-terms periods (report deadline and standstill) are not ends under the owner ruling of 2026-10-21. No held source shows an earlier revocation, supersession, expiry or termination. The first ending shown by held evidence is the express revocation by number in EO 12553, effective 1986-02-25. Later orders that merely recite or list this order would not be ends; none was found that does more than EO 12553.\",\"independent_command_check\":\"The order has no command independent of the board: the standstill is recited from the statute and depends on the board's creation and report. No other command in the order, and no other order that incorporates it, was found in the held texts.\",\"savings_check\":\"EO 12553 has no savings clause or exception (searched as described); the board's report and any settlement or later legislation are not obligations created by this order and are not claimed as surviving effects.\",\"scope_check\":\"Single clause, whole order. The dossier's inventory spans the recitals, the creation of the board, the report command and the statutory standstill recital to the signature; I confirm it as complete. EO 12553 revokes the whole order (it lists the order itself and its amendments), so no part of the order is left outside the ending.\"}","review_dependency_issues":[],"reviews":[{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/complete-v1/receipts/research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:9","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"},{"file_sha256":"5de0260d810af410db7ee710f4d0ceeb20eee3c8ac2ec307b317f9a0a261c311","path":"research/excavator/claude-apollo-2026-10-31/g07/packet/reviews/BLIND_REVIEW.jsonl","pointer":"line:9","review_kind":"independent_ai_source_review","scope":"whole_case","verdict":"upheld","version_binding":"exact"}],"scopes":[{"aligned_current_scopes":["EO:11027#whole"],"alignment":"exact_quote_and_source","different_current_fields":[],"scope_id":"EO:11027#whole"}],"source_pins":[{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761","state":"match"},"sha256":"945348afbba863ae7dc1fbe7df8759682fe1733962734a9255a1062c0561048c","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt","text_sha256":"6361bdc557b6acaeae6e6908cded18f4f8fef9fde1a97cc11fb7c12c8cdea761"},{"held_text":{"expected_sha256":"3815d9a6ef23e0e9c036e5d425958d6f192d4675b34a12839a40ebcbf54db755","path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11027.txt","sha256":"3815d9a6ef23e0e9c036e5d425958d6f192d4675b34a12839a40ebcbf54db755","state":"match"},"sha256":"d0eef9e177ce6fcc82784c16092569bc27bf8419870174461d11a1078c2b5e29","text_path":"jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_11027.txt","text_sha256":"3815d9a6ef23e0e9c036e5d425958d6f192d4675b34a12839a40ebcbf54db755"}]}]}

Status provisional NO, unchanged. The order creates a three-member board under section 10 of the Railway Labor Act to investigate the dispute between The Belt Railway Company of Chicago and employees represented by the Brotherhood of Locomotive Engineers ('I hereby create a board of three members', offset 1355 onward), directs it to report 'within thirty days from the date of-this order' (offset 1590 onward) and recites the section 10 status quo (offset 1866 onward). Deciding source: EO 12553 of 1986-02-25 (51 FR 7237, jobs/fr-originals/data/full_run/texts/EO_12553.txt), whose operative sentence (offsets 691-966) revokes 'the following Executive Orders, and any amendments thereto', and whose table names this order ('11040 Creating an emergency board to investigate a railroad labor dispute', offsets 18012-18085); no effective-date wording, so effective on signing 1986-02-25. The end cites the dossier's ACT record (agent-located Rastell record, independently AI-reviewed, effective_class on_signing). Report-deadline ruling (2026-10-21, option C) applied: the report deadline (1962-09-05 (signing date 1962-08-06 plus 30 days; counting convention not ruled)) and the report itself are spent facts, not ends. Spent facts disclosed under the report-deadline ruling (not ends): the held NMB copy of the board's report (jobs/source-ladder/output/texts/queue_EO_11040_Actual_report_date_of_the_board_created_by_EO_11040_Presidential_Emergency_Board_Rail_ed6de90c70.txt, https://nmb.gov/NMB_Application/wp-content/uploads/2025/05/PEB-150-Report.pdf, sha256 002c3eea...) is dated on its cover 'March 4, 1963' (offset 1112) and its letter of transmittal says the board 'has been able to bring about a voluntary negotiated settlement of the dispute by the parties'; the order is functionally spent on 1963-03-04 (report) and the section 10 status quo ran 30 days after the report (about 1963-04-03). Two discrepancies for the reviewer: the cover reads 'APPOINTED BY EXECUTIVE ORDER 11041' while the transmittal letter names 'Executive Order No. 11040' and the same date and parties, and the report date falls after the 30-day deadline with no held extension instrument. The transmittal letter's own day is OCR-garbled ('Ma~'ch /~, 1963'). The report text is not dossier evidence, so it is disclosed here rather than attached as a later_event; existing FETCH_QUEUE line 407 already requests this report-date source. D6 / later events: the dossier chain and actor provisions show only EO 12553; the draft's later-event and whole-order-end proposals (OFR disposition note 'Revoked by: EO 12553, February 25, 1986' and the 1989 Codification table 'Revoked by EO 12553') point to the same instrument and are attached as the end itself; a by-number scan of all held FR order texts and APP texts found no other naming instrument (EO_9289, EO_9602 and EO_12593 (FR page numbers 11040 in contents lists), EO_12553 (the revocation)). Quotations in this claim are copied from the held OCR or text layers with line breaks, soft hyphens and letter-spacing normalized; the exact spans are at the stated offsets. No conflicts are listed in the draft or dossier.
